## T-046 — DPAPI secret store wipes all stored secrets after a silent load failure (BUG-001, Critical)

- **Branch:** feat/dpapi-store-wipe (findings of the 2026-11-04 code review; full definitions and line references live in `docs/CODE_REVIEW.md`)
- **Origin:** the secret store is the last line of defense for user keys; a silent wipe destroys every stored secret on the first corrupt file - the exact scenario the trading-safety rule "never overwrite encrypted secrets when decryption fails" forbids.
- **What:** `BUG-001` - `WindowsDpapiSecretStore.LoadStoreAsync` logs and swallows every load failure (corrupt JSON, invalid base64, transient read error) and continues with an empty/partial `_cache`; the very next `SetAsync` -> `SaveStoreAsync` rewrites the whole file from that cache, permanently destroying all previously stored secrets. `InitializeAsync` never surfaces the failure.
- **Scope:** `src/ZettaTrade.Infrastructure/Secrets/WindowsDpapiSecretStore.cs` (a failure-surfacing path out of `InitializeAsync`; if an interface change is needed, escalate).
- **Acceptance:** a corrupt/truncated/unreadable store file fails loudly at initialization and is never overwritten by a subsequent `SetAsync`; secrets become unusable-not-lost (an explicit error surfaced, a logged condition); the happy path is unchanged.
- **Tests:** the load-failure matrix (missing / corrupt JSON / bad base64 / locked file) with the no-wipe invariant pinned (after a failed load, a `SetAsync` attempt leaves the file byte-identical and reports the error).
- **Status:** done. Canon: commit `13c8b86` is on feat/dpapi-store-wipe; the load-failure matrix (corrupt/truncated JSON, invalid base64, locked file) + the happy-path roundtrip pass and the Initialize failure surfaces loudly (the store then refuses all operations; the on-disk secrets are never overwritten). The optional explicit `storePath` constructor override is a test seam (the interface is unchanged). Wave note: the release version bump + CHANGELOG land once for the whole fixed-wave in a separate release branch after the merges — a supervisor-approved deviation from rule 6 for parallel work.

## T-047 — MCP server drops every response for numeric JSON-RPC ids (BUG-002, Critical)

- **Branch:** feat/mcp-numeric-ids (code review 2026-11-04; definitions in `docs/CODE_REVIEW.md`)
- **Origin:** every mainline MCP client numbers its JSON-RPC requests (`"id": 1, 2, ...`); against our server they all hang forever - the basic handshake with real clients is broken.
- **What:** `BUG-002` - the response id is parsed with `GetString()`, which returns null for numeric ids, so the `id != null` gate drops every response; compounded by the initialize reply hardcoding `id = "1"` instead of echoing the request's id.
- **Scope:** `src/ZettaTrade.AI/Mcp/McpServerHost.cs`.
- **Acceptance:** ids travel through unchanged: string, numeric and null ids all produce correctly matched replies; the initialize reply echoes the initialize request's id; a numeric-id client completes handshake + a tool call without hanging.
- **Tests:** the id-shape matrix (string / integer / null / missing) against the request loop; an end-to-end numeric-id round-trip (tools/call) with a scripted client.
- **Status:** done. Canon: commit `150c1b1` is on feat/mcp-numeric-ids; the gate prints `GATE PASSED` (baseline develop) and the scripted stdio harness carries 4 green facts: the string-id handshake (version/protocol/capabilities pinned, garbage lines only ever reach the logger), the full numeric round-trip (initialize id 1 + tools/call id 2, both echoed as numbers with the tool content verified), the explicit-null id answered with an echoed null, and the id-less notification processed but never answered (the trailing string-id request proves the loop read both lines in order). The id travels as a cloned raw `JsonElement` gated on presence, not on JSON null being falsy; a missing id stays a true notification. The real-client e2e probe (an external mainline MCP client) stays with merge review — the scripted in-process client stands in its place. Wave note: the release version bump + CHANGELOG land once for the whole fixed-wave in a separate release branch after the merges — a supervisor-approved deviation from rule 6 for parallel work.
- **Landed:** merged into develop as merge commit `f0379d8`; merge revisions: `McpServerHost` resolved to T-050's pipelines version wholesale (a superset carrying the spec error replies and the request-id echo), and the stdio test file is the union keeping T-050's 3-envelope handshake expectation, so an unparsable garbage line now answers `-32700` on stdout (BUG-056; this section's garbage-stays-off-stdout facet is superseded), while the id-shape matrix and the numeric round-trip remain as written above.

## T-048 — Bybit reconnect resubscribes cancel each other (reconnect churn with 2+ symbols) (BUG-003, Critical)

- **Branch:** feat/bybit-resubscribe (code review 2026-11-04; definitions in `docs/CODE_REVIEW.md`)
- **Origin:** after any network blip a user tracking 2+ symbols enters an unrecoverable reconnect loop with no market data until a restart - the feed degenerates to nothing silently.
- **What:** `BUG-003` - the resubscribe pipeline is `_resubscribeStreams.Select(...).Switch()`: a new emission disposes (cancels) the previous in-flight inner subscription. `HandleConnectionStateChanged` emits all tracked symbols synchronously in one `foreach` without awaiting, so with >= 2 symbols every resubscribe send but the last is cancelled mid-`SendAsync`; cancelling an in-flight send aborts the socket and re-triggers the reconnect. The same flaw rides the gap-recovery path (a new gap frame kills an in-flight resubscribe).
- **Scope:** `src/ZettaTrade.Infrastructure/Exchange/BybitAdapter.cs`.
- **Acceptance:** with 3 tracked symbols a reconnect resubscribes all three to completion (no in-flight send aborted); gap recovery serializes against in-flight resubscribes; no reconnect churn loop.
- **Tests:** a loopback WebSocket fake that counts overlapping `SendAsync` calls and records cancellations; the 2- and 3-symbol reconnect scenarios; a gap-frame-during-resubscribe scenario.
- **Status:** done. Canon: commit `ac7bca2` is on feat/bybit-resubscribe; the gate prints `GATE PASSED` (baseline develop); 3 new facts over the loopback Kestrel server (the 2- and 3-symbol reconnect scenarios resubscribe every tracked symbol to the exact frame counts with the connection pinned on the recovered session - one ticker per session per symbol proves the topics survived - and the gap-during-resubscribe scenario proves two gap deltas plus the burst complete with zero re-subscribe failures logged, both books rebuilt from the recovery snapshots). The fix replaces the `_resubscribeStreams.Subject + Select/FromAsync/Catch/Switch` pipeline with an unbounded `Channel<Symbol>` served by ONE tracked consumer task (`RunResubscribeLoopAsync`, awaited on disposal like the keepalive loop): requests are honored FIFO and every send runs to completion - a newer request (a reconnect burst, a gap frame) queues behind and never cancels an in-flight send, because a cancelled send aborts the whole socket and churns the reconnect; per-request failure logging stays local, and disposal cancellation is unchanged.
- **Wave note:** the release version bump + CHANGELOG land once for the whole fixed-wave in a separate release branch after the merges - a supervisor-approved deviation from rule 6 for parallel work (same as T-040/T-047/T-050).
- **Landed:** merged into develop as merge commit `6ad770c`, with the wave merge commit `cbd5dbb` folding in the depth adaptation: the resubscribe facts subscribe with the explicit `DepthLevelCount = 50`, because T-052's strict depth contract (BUG-032, Bybit serves {1, 50}) rejects the default-20 book subscribe the pre-merge facts used.

## T-049 — Secret stores: silent data loss and rule violations across platforms (BUG-009, BUG-010, BUG-011, BUG-036...BUG-041)

- **Branch:** feat/secrets-stores (base `develop`; queued 2026-11-04, code review 2026-11-04; definitions in `docs/CODE_REVIEW.md`)
- **Origin:** the stores share the files, so one branch fixes the whole surface; two findings violate standing trading-safety rules (secrets via stdin only; never overwrite on a failed write), the rest are silent data loss on all platforms.
- **What:**
  - `BUG-009` - macOS keychain passes the secret as a command-line argument (`"-w", value`), exposing it in the process list; `-w` without a value does read stdin (the comment claiming otherwise is factually wrong).
  - `BUG-010` - DPAPI `SaveStoreAsync` swallows all write errors while `SetAsync`/`RemoveAsync` report success: a failed write leaves the secret memory-only; a deletion is silently undone after restart.
  - `BUG-011` - DPAPI `ListKeysAsync` blocks synchronously on `_lock.Wait(ct)` inside an async contract method: freeze/deadlock risk while a write holds the semaphore across file I/O.
  - `BUG-036` - `ISecretStore.ListKeysAsync` always returns an empty list on macOS and Linux.
  - `BUG-037` - on timeout/cancellation the `security`/`secret-tool` child process is never killed (`using Process` disposes the handle, not the process): an orphan that may hold a keychain prompt.
  - `BUG-038` - the keychain update is a non-atomic destroy-then-create: a failure between the steps loses the old secret; the empty `catch { }` swallows real keychain errors.
  - `BUG-039` - DPAPI `InitializeAsync` -> `LoadStoreAsync` reassigns `_cache` without holding `_lock`: a concurrent early `SetAsync` is clobbered and then erased from disk by the next save.
  - `BUG-040` - DPAPI `GetAsync` returns null on any decryption failure, conflating "corrupt/unreadable" with "missing".
  - `BUG-041` - `EncryptedFileSecretStore` on a truncated file silently "starts fresh" and the next `SetAsync` overwrites the undecryptable file (fails the never-overwrite rule; the content is unrecoverable either way).
- **Scope:** `src/ZettaTrade.Infrastructure/Secrets/` (`WindowsDpapiSecretStore.cs`, `MacOSKeychainSecretStore.cs`, `LinuxSecretServiceStore.cs`, `EncryptedFileSecretStore.cs`).
- **Acceptance:** secrets travel via stdin only; write failures surface (no swallowed success); `ListKeysAsync` works on all platforms; children die on timeout; the update path is atomic or fails with the old secret intact; decrypt failures are distinguishable from missing keys.
- **Tests:** per-bug invariant assertions; the never-overwrite invariant for the DPAPI + encrypted-file pair; platform guards documented (Windows tests run everywhere; macOS/Linux paths skipped with the guard noted).
- **Status:** done. Canon: commit `05e6a1f` is on feat/secrets-stores; full Infrastructure.Tests suite passes 121/121 (no warnings; the new secrets suites hold 13 facts) and gate prints `GATE PASSED`. Shipped: BUG-009 — the macOS secret value travels via stdin (`add-generic-password ... -w` with no value, `stdinData: value + "\n"`, `RedirectStandardInput` on), never as a process argument; BUG-038 — the update path is the single `add-generic-password -U` call (the standalone destroy-then-create and its empty `catch { }` are deleted); BUG-010 — DPAPI `SaveStoreAsync` propagates write/removal-save failures (locked-file scenarios throw `UnauthorizedAccessException` instead of claiming success; the store stays healthy once writable again); BUG-039 — `InitializeAsync` holds `_lock` around `LoadStoreAsync`, so an early concurrent `SetAsync` applies on top of the loaded state and survives the next save; BUG-011 — `ListKeysAsync` is `async` and awaits `_lock.WaitAsync(ct)` (no synchronous wait in an async contract method); BUG-040 — a decryption failure throws `InvalidOperationException` ("NOT a missing key") while the key stays listed; BUG-036 — `ListKeysAsync` works on all platforms: macOS parses `security dump-keychain`'s attribute dump (records end at `class:` lines; service-match + `acct` buffered per record), Linux parses `secret-tool search --all service <Service>`'s blank-line-separated blocks (service-match + the `key` attribute); both parsers are pure functions in the shared unattributed `SecretListOutputParsers` so tests cover them everywhere (a class-level `[SupportedOSPlatform]` broke CA1416 from test calls); BUG-037 — `SecretProcessGuard.KillUnfinished(process)` runs in a catch-rethrow around the child invocation in both `Run*` methods (orphan-children die with their invocation); BUG-041 — `EncryptedFileSecretStore` treats a too-short file (`< NonceSizeBytes + TagSizeBytes`) as truncated content: initialization throws, the file stays byte-identical and no `SetAsync` ever overwrites it; the missing-FILE happy path is unchanged, and the store constructor gained an optional explicit `filePath` test seam (mirrors the T-046 DPAPI seam precedent).
- **Merge revision:** none expected — T-046's DPAPI load-failure hardening is already merged into the develop base, so `WindowsDpapiSecretStore` carries no 3-way merge; the two new shared files (`SecretProcessGuard`, `SecretListOutputParsers`) are additive.
- **Wave note:** the release version bump + CHANGELOG land once for the whole fixed-wave in a separate release branch after the merges — a supervisor-approved deviation from rule 6 for parallel work.
- **Landed:** merged into develop as merge commit `8110835`.

## T-050 — MCP pipelines: host lock stall, client id matching, unread stderr, tools defaults (BUG-007, BUG-008, BUG-020...BUG-024, BUG-056)

- **Branch:** feat/mcp-pipelines (code review 2026-11-04; definitions in `docs/CODE_REVIEW.md`)
- **Origin:** the host and the client are the AI integration's two halves and the tools are its trading surface; the tools findings drop the "never substitute defaults for unparsable enums" rule - invalid orders flow toward execution.
- **What:**
  - `BUG-007` - `TradingToolsRegistrar` parses side/type with `Enum.TryParse`: numeric and undefined values pass (`"999"` -> `(OrderSide)999`), and `OrderRequest.Validate` never checks enum definedness either (a Core concern if it needs a contract change - escalate).
  - `BUG-008` - an unparsable `timeInForce` silently defaults to `GTC`; an unparsable `timeframe` silently defaults to `H1` (the model asks M1 and gets H1 candles).
  - `BUG-020` - the host holds `_lock` across `await tool.Handler(...)` (which may await a confirmation dialog): `RegisterTool`/`RegisterResource`/`RegisterPrompt` and `tools/list` block synchronously on `_lock.Wait()` - a UI-thread registration stalls until the tool call completes (deadlock risk).
  - `BUG-021` - the client's stderr drain is a fire-and-forget `_ = Task.Run(...)`: its exceptions are unobserved, and once the task dies the child's stderr pipe can fill and block the server process.
  - `BUG-022` - reconnecting while connected overwrites `_serverProcess`/`_httpClient` without killing the previous child (a leaked process); `DisposeAsync` calls `Kill` on a process that never started (`InvalidOperationException` thrown out of disposal).
  - `BUG-023` - the stdio reply is never matched to the request id (a random Guid); any interleaved server line (notification/log) desynchronizes the stream; a cancelled `ReadLineAsync` leaves a half-consumed line corrupting subsequent reads.
  - `BUG-024` - HTTP auth resolves `AuthSecretRef` to a hardcoded `"placeholder-token"` Bearer header instead of the secret store (HTTP authentication never works; secrets bypass `ISecretStore`).
  - `BUG-056` - malformed requests (missing `method`/`params.name`, bad JSON) and unknown methods with an id produce no JSON-RPC error reply (the client waits forever); `StopAsync` cancels and immediately disposes `_cts` while the run loop may sit blocked in `Console.In.ReadLineAsync`.
- **Scope:** `src/ZettaTrade.AI/Mcp/` (`McpServerHost.cs`, `McpClientService.cs`, `Tools/TradingToolsRegistrar.cs`), `src/ZettaTrade.Core/Entities/OrderRequest.cs`.
- **Acceptance:** no defaults for unparsable/undefined enums (an explicit error per the rule); the host lock is held only over synchronous work (registrations never wait on tool calls); replies are matched by id with desync tolerance; the stderr drain is lifespan-owned; reconnect leaks no child process and disposal never throws; malformed requests get error replies; HTTP auth uses `ISecretStore`.
- **Tests:** a scripted host client (registration while a tool call is in flight), a client framing/desync simulation, the tools parse-rejection matrix (`999`, undefined names, malformed JSON), the HTTP-transport secret path with a fake store.
- **Status:** done. Canon: commit `1441934` is on feat/mcp-pipelines; the gate prints `GATE PASSED` (baseline develop); 26 new facts (the tools parse matrix 9, the host error matrix + the parked-handler registration fact 7, the client framing/secrets/reconnect facts 7) plus 3 Core `OrderRequest.Validate` definedness facts (its suite 10/10), and the stdio purity fact now expects 3 envelopes. The tools parse side/orderType only from defined members (`ToolEnumParser` + `AllowedNames`), error on a PROVIDED unparsable timeInForce/timeframe and default exactly on omission; `OrderRequest.Validate` refuses undefined side/type/time-in-force toward execution. The host replies -32700/-32600/-32601/-32602 by request shape (parse errors ride id null, notifications stay silent), holds its lock only over the synchronous table read (a parked tool handler never blocks a registration - BUG-020), logs parse failures to the pipeline and waits bounded in `StopAsync`; the client matches replies by request id with framing tolerance (garbage and stranger lines are skipped, BUG-023), keeps the stderr drain owned and observed (BUG-021), sends the `ISecretStore` Bearer never a placeholder (loopback HTTP stub; a missing secret connects unauthenticated, BUG-024), tears the previous connection down before reconnecting and disposes a never-started client silently (BUG-022). Found along the tests: the args-to-DTO binding was case-sensitive against the camelCase schema keys (the simplest honest call answered "Symbol is required") - fixed with case-insensitive `ToolArgsOptions` riding the camel naming policy. Test infra: the two Console-owning suites (stdio purity, host errors) share one xUnit collection so the process-wide Console primitives never overlap.
- **Merge revision:** T-047's pushed `McpServerHostStdioTests.cs` rewrite asserts garbage is SILENT on stdout (2 envelopes); after both branches merge the first stdio fact must keep T-050's 3-envelope expectation - the parse-error reply IS protocol JSON and the honest behavior (BUG-056) wins; the conflicting hunks sit in `Handshake_StdoutCarriesProtocolJsonOnly`'s count/assembles block.
- **Wave note:** the release version bump + CHANGELOG land once for the whole fixed-wave in a separate release branch after the merges - a supervisor-approved deviation from rule 6 for parallel work (same as T-040/T-047).
- **Landed:** merged into develop as merge commit `12c8141`; merge revisions: `McpServerHost` = this branch's version wholesale (see T-047's Landed note), the stdio test file is the union absorbing T-047's facts, and the wave merge commit `cbd5dbb` folded in the notification-silence junction: a request with no id now answers nothing on the normal-method path too (`response = null` after the switch where the pipelines host had guarded only the error branches).

## T-051 — Paper trading ledger: corrupted entry price, stale reference price, phantom fills (BUG-005, BUG-006, BUG-033...BUG-035)

- **Branch:** feat/indicator-scan (base `develop`; queued 2026-11-04, code review 2026-11-04; definitions in `docs/CODE_REVIEW.md`)
- **Origin:** the paper adapter is the trading simulator behind every demo user; its average entry and unrealized PnL are the numbers the user acts on, and every defect here corrupts exactly them (or lies about a rejection).
- **What:**
  - `BUG-005` - the net-position branch treats any same-sign fill as "extending": a reducing fill is re-averaged with the wrong denominator (long 1 @ 100 -> sell 0.5 @ 150 leaves entry 350 for the remaining 0.5 instead of 100), corrupting average entry and unrealized PnL on every partial close.
  - `BUG-006` - the reference price takes `bars[^1]` of a from-anchored `limit: 1` window (`now - 24h`): on ascending-order backends (Binance klines oldest-first) that is a ~24-hour-old close, so paper market fills and limit-crossing decisions use a day-old price; Bybit/Demo differ silently.
  - `BUG-033` - `FillAndApply` records the Filled order into `_orders` before the balance validation inside `ApplyFillToPortfolio`: a balance-rejected order leaves a phantom Filled record while the caller receives an exception.
  - `BUG-034` - the crossing-zero branch opens a fresh opposite position at the fill price but never resets `OpenedAt`: the new position carries the closed one's timestamp.
  - `BUG-035` - `CancelOrderCoreAsync` waits on `_tradingLock.WaitAsync()` without the caller's `ct`: a wedged lock holder blocks cancellation indefinitely.
- **Scope:** `src/ZettaTrade.Infrastructure/Exchange/PaperTradingAdapter.cs`.
- **Acceptance:** a reducing fill never changes the average entry of the remaining size; the reference close is the newest bar on every backend (an explicit ordering contract with the adapters, e.g. a max-window fetch and a latest-close selection); a balance rejection persists no Filled order; a fresh position carries its own fill-time `OpenedAt`; cancel respects the caller's cancellation.
- **Tests:** the ledger scenarios (extend / partial close / cross-zero / balance rejection) against an ascending and a descending history fake; the cancel-with-cancelled-token scenario.
- **Status:** done. Canon: commit `e9d7fdc` is on feat/indicator-scan; full Infrastructure.Tests suite passes 115/115 (no warnings; the paper suite now holds 23 facts) and gate prints `GATE PASSED`. Shipped: BUG-005 — `NetPositionFromFill` distinguishes extending from reducing: a same-sign fill smaller than the net exposure closes part of the position and leaves the average entry of the remainder intact (extension keeps the weighted average over the whole new size); BUG-006 — `ResolveReferenceCloseAsync` fetches the whole lookback window (`ReferenceBarFetchLimit = lookback hours + 1`, replacing the fixed `limit: 1`) and selects the newest bar BY ITS `Time` via a fold, never by list position, so ascending (Binance) and descending (Bybit/Demo) backends agree on the newest close; the by-time ordering contract is documented on the method (the Core `IMarketDataAdapter` interface is untouched — supervisor-owned); BUG-033 — `FillAndApply` applies the portfolio BEFORE recording the order, so a balance rejection throws and leaves no phantom Filled record (the private `_orders` store is the probe: pre-fix it kept the ghost); BUG-034 — the cross-zero branch stamps the fresh opposite position with its own fill-time `OpenedAt`; BUG-035 — `CancelOrderAsync` passes the caller's `ct` into `_tradingLock.WaitAsync(ct)` (acquisition outside the try/release, so a cancelled wait never releases an un-acquired semaphore). New facts: the partial-close entry invariance (+balance), the extension weighted average, the asc/desc newest-close pair (+ the full-window fetched assertion), the no-phantom after rejection (+ adapter stays usable), the cross-zero fresh stamp, and the cancelled-token cancel against a wedged lock (reflection-held `SemaphoreSlim`, the BUG-011 smoke precedent).
- **Merge revision:** none expected — the ledger math rework is contained in one file with no nearby develop-side changes.
- **Wave note:** the release version bump + CHANGELOG land once for the whole fixed-wave in a separate release branch after the merges — a supervisor-approved deviation from rule 6 for parallel work.
- **Landed:** merged into develop as merge commit `9b981bd` (the feat/indicator-scan branch carried T-051 through together with T-053 and T-054).

## T-052 — WebSocket transport: dead feed after reconnect, send races, frame parser, ignored depth (BUG-004, BUG-027...BUG-032)

- **Branch:** feat/ws-transport (base `develop`; queued 2026-11-04, code review 2026-11-04; definitions in `docs/CODE_REVIEW.md`)
- **Origin:** the transport is the market-data lifeline; the BUG-004 defect silences every feed permanently after the first transient disconnect - the worst failure class in a trading terminal (stale prices presented as live).
- **What:**
  - `BUG-004` - the background loop silently reconnects the socket but nothing ever re-sends the `SUBSCRIBE` frames (stream starts run only on new Rx outer subscriptions): after any transient disconnect the ticker/depth/trade feeds of all subscribed symbols go silent forever while the Rx subscriptions stay alive; a subscriber disposing mid-start cancels the start's send token and aborts the shared socket, making the feed unrecoverable.
  - `BUG-027` - the Bybit trade-frame parser calls `TryGetProperty("S")` before the `ValueKind != Object` check: a non-object array entry throws `InvalidOperationException` and aborts all remaining trades of the frame instead of being counted as dropped (contradicting the method's own doc).
  - `BUG-028` - `SubscribeSymbolAsync`/`UnsubscribeSymbolAsync` send without the `_streamStartGate` used by `StartSymbolStreamsAsync`: an ungated unsubscribe concurrent with a gated subscribe produces two simultaneous `ClientWebSocket.SendAsync` calls on one socket -> `InvalidOperationException` ("there is already one outstanding send").
  - `BUG-029` - `ConnectAsync` mutates the shared `_cts` (`Cancel`/`Dispose`/replace) with no synchronization: overlapping calls corrupt the session token (a leaked CTS, or the old loop's `Task.Delay` dies as `ObjectDisposedException`); `_reconnectAttempts` resets only on a successful open, so a failed previous session shrinks the next one's attempt budget.
  - `BUG-030` - `DisconnectAsync` disposes `_webSocket` while the receive loop may be blocked in `ReceiveAsync`: the resulting `ObjectDisposedException` matches none of the loop's inner catches and escapes as a bogus "background loop crashed" error.
  - `BUG-031` - `DemoMarketDataAdapter` increments `++_tradeSequence` on concurrent per-symbol timer threads: lost increments, duplicate `demo-...-{sequence}` trade ids, parity flips of side/price.
  - `BUG-032` - `ExchangeAdapterBase.SubscribeOrderBook(symbol, depth)` silently ignores `depth`: Binance pins 20 levels, Bybit 50; a caller requesting 5 or 100 gets the fixed depth with no error.
- **Scope:** `src/ZettaTrade.Infrastructure/Exchange/` (`BinanceAdapter.cs`, `WebSocketClient.cs`, `BybitAdapter.cs`, `ExchangeAdapterBase.cs`), `src/ZettaTrade.Infrastructure/Demo/DemoMarketDataAdapter.cs`.
- **Acceptance:** a killed-and-rejoined connection resubscribes everything (no silent feed); all socket sends serialize behind one gate; the connect/disconnect lifecycle is race-free and free of bogus errors; malformed trade frames are counted and skipped, never thrown; the depth contract is explicit (honored via a seam change - escalate if `IMarketDataAdapter` needs an interface change - or rejected with a clear error, never a silent default).
- **Tests:** loopback-WS scenarios (drop, rejoin, a resubscribe storm, overlapping sends), the frame-parser matrix, the depth contract test, a demo counter concurrency test.
- **Status:** done. Canon: commit `7ce1e0a` is on feat/ws-transport; full Infrastructure.Tests suite passes 122/122 (no warnings) and gate prints `GATE PASSED`. Shipped: BUG-004 — Binance now owns a Bybit-style tracked-symbols reconnect re-subscribe (a `Channel<Symbol>` queue served by one FIFO consumer task whose sends ride the adapter-owned `_lifetimeCts`, never a caller's token) and `ExchangeAdapterBase` documents the send-vs-start-token contract; BUG-027 — the object-shape check precedes `TryGetProperty("S")`, malformed entries are counted and dropped (never thrown); BUG-028 — every Binance socket send (stream start, public subscribe/unsubscribe, reconnect re-subscribe) serializes behind `_streamStartGate`; BUG-029/BUG-030 — `WebSocketClient` serializes the whole connect/disconnect lifecycle behind a `_lifecycleGate` semaphore, tears sessions down cancel-first (await loop → close/dispose socket → conditional state emission), starts every session on a fresh attempt budget, and gains the missing OCE catch in the background loop; BUG-031 — the demo trade sequence increments via `Interlocked.Increment`; BUG-032 — the explicit depth contract rides the new base seam `StartSymbolStreamsAsync(Symbol, int? bookDepth, ct)` + `protected abstract IReadOnlySet<int> SupportedBookDepths` (Binance serves {5, 10, 20}, Bybit serves {1, 50}): a supported depth goes on the wire verbatim, anything else errors on subscribe via the subscription pipeline, never a silent default, and no `IMarketDataAdapter` interface change was needed. New tests: `BinanceAdapterResubscribeTests` (2/3-symbol reconnect frame counts, the depth-5 wire fact, overlapping public calls), 3 new `WebSocketClientTests` facts (overlapping connects serialize, no spurious loop crash on disconnect, a fresh attempt budget per session), 3 new Bybit facts (malformed-entry counting, the orderbook.1 wire fact, the {1, 50} rejection), the base depth facts in `ExchangeAdapterBaseSubscriptionTests`, the demo 12-trade sequence-uniqueness fact.
- **Merge revision:** `BybitAdapter` will need a 3-way merge with T-048's resubscribe churn fix (this branch keeps the OLD Subject+Switch pipeline on the Bybit side and does not take BUG-003 here; the Bybit start send rides `_lifetimeCts` here); accepted UI escalation for the supervisor: `OrderBookViewModel` calls `SubscribeOrderBook(symbol!)` with the interface default of 20, which the `orderbook.1`-only Bybit topic set now rejects on subscribe — `GuardStreamFailures` logs an error and serves an empty book (the switch chain survives); the UI-side depth fix is deliberately out of this task's scope.
- **Wave note:** the release version bump + CHANGELOG land once for the whole fixed-wave in a separate release branch after the merges — a supervisor-approved deviation from rule 6 for parallel work.
- **Landed:** merged into develop as merge commit `ffcfad3`; the Bybit overlap bullet above resolved in the wave merge (BUG-032's depth contract and T-048's FIFO resubscribe pipeline coexist; the test-side adaptation is noted under T-048).

## T-053 — Settings service: reload races erase just-written values, changes never published (BUG-012, BUG-013, BUG-045)

- **Branch:** feat/indicator-scan (base `develop`; queued 2026-11-04, code review 2026-11-04; definitions in `docs/CODE_REVIEW.md`)
- **Origin:** the settings service is the single persistence interface for every workspace preference; the reload race silently loses user edits - the most painful silently-losing bug inside a trading terminal.
- **What:**
  - `BUG-012` - `LoadFromFileAsync` mutates the shared `_settings` (`Clear()` + repopulate) without `_saveLock`: a debounced external reload interleaved with `SetAsync`/`SaveAsync` erases just-written values and the next save persists the stale snapshot (lost writes).
  - `BUG-013` - the external reload replaces values but never calls `_changes.OnNext`: every `Changes` subscriber keeps operating on stale values (the promised hot-reload contract never reaches consumers).
  - `BUG-045` - the change payload mixes types (`OldValue` boxed `JsonElement?` vs a typed `NewValue`), so subscribers comparing both always mis-handle one side; the `_isSaving` guard resets in `finally` before the `File.Move` event is delivered, so every save can self-trigger a reload and widen the BUG-012 race.
- **Scope:** `src/ZettaTrade.Infrastructure/Configuration/SettingsService.cs` (+ the change payload type; keep `SettingsChange` backward compatible or escalate).
- **Acceptance:** external reloads serialize with saves (no lost writes); every external change publishes a well-typed `SettingsChange`; a save never self-triggers its own reload (the guard covers the whole delivery window); old and new values share one type.
- **Tests:** the lost-write race repro pinned first, then the fix; the change-publication assertions; the save-produces-zero-reload-events assertion.
- **Status:** done. Canon: commit `c70ad1e` is on feat/indicator-scan; full Infrastructure.Tests suite passes 123/123 (no warnings; the new settings suite holds 8 facts, the race fact stress-run green x4) and gate prints `GATE PASSED`. Shipped: BUG-012 — the WHOLE mutation set is serialized behind `_saveLock`: `LoadFromFileAsync` takes the lock around parse/apply (the pre-fix unlock `Clear()`+repopulate erased just-written values), and, the deeper hole the first review pass found, `SetAsync`'s memory mutation moved under the very same lock together with its save (the pre-fix unlocked `AddOrUpdate` left a window where a reload reloaded the STALE file over the fresh memory and the queued save persisted the stale snapshot); BUG-013 — every loaded snapshot now publishes one `SettingsChange` per difference (`ApplyAndCollect` diffs old/new by `JsonElement.DeepEquals`, applies, then publishes AFTER the lock release so a re-entering subscriber can never find the save lock held); BUG-045 — payloads are one-typed per event: a typed `SetAsync` reports the old value deserialized to the SAME `T` (a history value that fails to deserialize degrades to null, no substituted defaults), a reload publishes the file model's `JsonElement` for both sides (an absent side is null); the timing-guard `_isSaving` is replaced by a byte-exact `_lastSavedContent` fingerprint armed only after a successful write — a file event carrying the save's own content is skipped on load regardless of WHEN it lands, so the guard covers the whole delivery window (the pre-fix flag reset before the `File.Move` event was ever delivered). New facts: the lost-write race (reload racing set; the value survives in memory AND the follow-up save persists it), two concurrent sets both surviving serialized, one-event-per-difference reload publication (changed/added/removed), the full watcher pipeline surfacing an external rewrite within the debounce window, the typed old/new pair, the absent old side on a first set, and the zero-echo save assertion. `SettingsChange` itself is untouched (`object?` shape = backward compatible; all current App consumers stay `OfType<T>`-guarded — reload payloads degrade to filtered-out events there).
- **Merge revision:** consumer-side follow-up (escalation note, NOT in scope): App ViewModels currently drop reload-published values (`OfType<bool/string>` filters, `ApplyMarksChange`'s `is not string` guard) — genuinely hot-applying them needs the VMs to handle `JsonElement` payloads or re-read via `ReloadAsync`; the publication and typing contract is ready for that.
- **Wave note:** the release version bump + CHANGELOG land once for the whole fixed-wave in a separate release branch after the merges — a supervisor-approved deviation from rule 6 for parallel work.
- **Landed:** merged into develop as merge commit `9b981bd` (carried with T-051 and T-054).

## T-054 — Indicator plugin scan aborts on a plugin with missing dependencies (BUG-014)

- **Branch:** feat/indicator-scan (base `develop`; queued 2026-11-04, code review 2026-11-04; definitions in `docs/CODE_REVIEW.md`)
- **Origin:** the documented plugin-loader contract says a malformed plugin is skipped with a logged warning and the chart keeps working; with a missing dependent assembly the scan instead throws - the whole indicator seam degrades into a startup failure.
- **What:** `BUG-014` - `assembly.GetTypes()` is called outside the try (which only wraps `LoadFrom`): a plugin DLL whose dependencies are missing throws `ReflectionTypeLoadException` out of the catalog scan instead of skipping the file.
- **Scope:** `src/ZettaTrade.Infrastructure/Indicators/PluginIndicatorCatalog.cs`.
- **Acceptance:** a plugin that fails reflection logs its warning and is excluded; the remaining plugins of the directory still load; the scan itself never throws.
- **Tests:** extend the loader matrix with a dependency-missing plugin fixture (the garbage-dll case exists; this one covers the `GetTypes()` unwrap step).
- **Status:** done. Canon: commit `55a7da7` is on feat/indicator-scan; full Infrastructure.Tests suite passes 125/125 (no warnings; the indicator matrix now holds 7 facts) and gate prints `GATE PASSED`. Shipped: `ScanAssembly` guards the reflection unwrap step the pre-fix code ran unguarded — `assembly.GetTypes()` wrapped in its own try with a specific `ReflectionTypeLoadException` catch (warn-and-skip the FILE, disposing the LoaderExceptions with it) plus a generic catch for any other inspection failure, and a per-type try around the registration loop so even a partially broken type that `GetTypes()` returned skips alone while the file's scan continues (the healthy neighbors keep registering). Fixture: `tests/ZettaTrade.Plugins.BrokenBase` + `tests/ZettaTrade.Plugins.Broken` — a valid plugin assembly whose base class lives in a dependency assembly DELIBERATELY never staged next to it (the test project references it compile-only, and the fixture deletes any leaked copy from the test output), so `GetTypes()` genuinely throws `ReflectionTypeLoadException` at scan time; the dll is located BY NAME in the test output (typeof on the fixture's own type would raise the very failure under test). New facts: the dependency-missing plugin warns (`fail to load`) and is skipped while SMA and the garbage neighbor keep working, and the healthy SMA assembly never shows up in a single warning (per-file precision).
- **Merge revision:** supervisor action needed (forced, see the [PROTECTED] gate rule): add `tests/ZettaTrade.Plugins.Broken/ZettaTrade.Plugins.Broken.csproj` and `tests/ZettaTrade.Plugins.BrokenBase/ZettaTrade.Plugins.BrokenBase.csproj` to `ZettaTrade.slnx` — the solution file is supervisor-only, so the attempt was reverted and the projects still build/run as transitive ProjectReferences of the test project (the facts execute through the slnx membership of Infrastructure.Tests; only `dotnet format sln` does not lint the two fixture files).
- **Landed:** the suspended slnx registration was completed later by the supervisor's request, after solution-scoped (IDE) restores on a fresh checkout hit `Unable to find project information` on the two fixture files — canon: commit `641a226` on develop (slnx carries both fixtures in the `/tests/` folder; full build + 714/714 tests + `GATE PASSED`). The merge-revision bullet above records the at-merge-time state.
- **Wave note:** the release version bump + CHANGELOG land once for the whole fixed-wave in a separate release branch after the merges — a supervisor-approved deviation from rule 6 for parallel work.
- **Landed:** merged into develop as merge commit `9b981bd` (carried with T-051 and T-053; the supervisor-side slnx fixture registration from the merge bullet above stays a pending item).

## T-055 — Chart VM: persisted 12-hour clock lost in the common restore path + symbols bootstrap race (BUG-015, BUG-016)

- **Branch:** feat/xdg-paths (base `develop`; queued 2026-11-04, code review 2026-11-04; definitions in `docs/CODE_REVIEW.md`)
- **Origin:** every restart with the default `H1` time frame silently reverts the clock format the user configured; and rapid exchange switches can land the toolbar on a stale symbol list and overwrite an explicit user pick during a fetch.
- **What:**
  - `BUG-015` - `InitializeAsync` restores the persisted clock setting only at the tail of the time-frame restore path; the early returns (no stored TF, unparsable TF, restored == default `H1` - the common case) skip it: the chart silently re-renders the 24-hour clock on every restart.
  - `BUG-016` - `BootstrapSymbols` has no epoch/guard: every `AdapterChanges` emission starts an independent fetch and last-completed delivery wins `AvailableSymbols`/`SelectedSymbol`; two rapid exchange switches deliver out of order and the old exchange's list clobbers the new one's; a user pick made during the fetch is overwritten by the stale persisted value read afterwards.
- **Scope:** `src/ZettaTrade.App/ViewModels/ChartViewModel.cs`.
- **Acceptance:** the persisted clock is applied in every restore path (no-TF / unparsable / default `H1` / custom alike); an exchange switch lands on the newest fetch regardless of delivery order and never overwrites an explicit user pick (the epoch/guard pattern the chart's older-history machinery already uses).
- **Tests:** the restore matrix (no TF / unparsable / default `H1` / custom); the switch-race scenario (two reversed fetch completions + a mid-fetch user pick).
- **Status:** done. Canon: commit `ab1ef7a` is on feat/xdg-paths; full App.Tests suite passes 302/302 (no warnings; the chart facts now hold 70, of which 5 are new) and gate prints `GATE PASSED`. Shipped: BUG-015 — the persisted clock restore moved OFF the tail of the time-frame restore to the top of `InitializeAsync` (it is an independent pure consumer of its setting): all four restore paths — no persisted TF, unparsable TF, stored TF equal to the default H1 (the common case), custom TF — now apply `UI.ChartUse12HourClock`, the old tail assignment deleted; BUG-016 — `BootstrapSymbols` claims an epoch per emission (`Interlocked` counter `_symbolBootstrapEpoch`, started-fresh snapshot `selectionAtStart`): every `ApplySymbolBootstrap` delivery first checks `Volatile.Read(epoch) == its epoch` and a superseded delivery LOGS and drops before it can touch `AvailableSymbols`/marks/selection — an old exchange's list never lands after a newer switch; the pick part — `LoadBootstrapAsync` re-projected with `selectionAtStart`: a symbol picked while the fetch was in flight supersedes the read-before-fetch persisted preference (`pickedDuringFetch` defers the parsed preference — it stays STORED and keeps its first choice on the next unpicked switch), so the stale snapshot never overwrites an explicit user decision. The parenthetical acceptance wording is honored as the same epoch scheme the older-history machinery runs (a CTS recycled per rebase) stated on a counter: dropping on delivery, not cancelling the fetch, keeps the provider cache warm for whichever exchange owns the workspace next. New facts: the restore matrix — no-TF / unparsable / default-H1 each apply the persisted clock (and none rewrites it; the custom path was already pinned) — plus the reversed-completions drop (a stale-epoch delivery fabricates the real switch repro: a real `SwitchToAsync` then an older-epoch delivery of the OTHER exchange's list — dropped, rows and selection untouched), the mid-flight pick (the persistence read before the fetch window parked through a `TaskCompletionSource` gate on the fake's `GetSymbolsAsync`, the pick lands inside it: `PersistedDeferred` true, the explicit pick owned by the delivery) and the no-pick complement (the persisted preference stays first choice). Tests seam reworked: the old `ApplyBootstrapForFactsAsync` split into `StartBootstrapForFactsAsync` (real start, epoch bump, applies its own epoch), `ApplyBootstrapForFactsAsync(adapter, epoch)` (a caller-declared delivery for the stale repro) and `LoadBootstrapForFactsAsync` (the load half alone — no epoch, nothing applied); the `SymbolBootstrap` record went `internal` with the added `PersistedDeferred` flag (a deferral is not a fall-through — `LogPersistedSymbolOutcome` tells it apart from the not-listed/hidden warnings).
- **Merge revision:** none expected — the changes are contained in the one ViewModel file plus its fact file; the App-side consumers of the bootstrap record are chart-internal.
- **Wave note:** the release version bump + CHANGELOG land once for the whole fixed-wave in a separate release branch after the merges — a supervisor-approved deviation from rule 6 for parallel work.
- **Landed:** merged into develop as merge commit `cbd5dbb` (the whole feat/xdg-paths wave rides one merge); shipped as `0.1.0-alpha.22` on `release/alpha.22-27` (see CHANGELOG.md).

## T-056 — App VM error paths: unlogged startup crash, silent order failures, off-thread and inconsistent UI (BUG-017...BUG-019, BUG-053...BUG-055)

- **Branch:** feat/xdg-paths (base `develop`; queued 2026-11-04, code review 2026-11-04; definitions in `docs/CODE_REVIEW.md`)
- **Origin:** all six findings share one failure class: the user cannot see what went wrong, or the UI quietly misrepresents state/time; in a trading terminal, silent failure is the costliest class after data loss.
- **What:**
  - `BUG-017` - the order dialog catches only `InvalidOperationException`, but `PlaceOrderAsync` can throw network exceptions via the paper adapter's reference-bar REST call; they escape to `ReactiveCommand.ThrownExceptions`, which nothing observes: the order is silently not placed (no message, no journal entry).
  - `BUG-018` - the StatusBar fallback-notice `Observable.Timer(...)` callback runs on a ThreadPool thread and sets the UI-bound `ConnectionStatus` without `ObserveOnUiThread` (reached when the persisted exchange id is unregistered at startup).
  - `BUG-019` - `OnFrameworkInitializationCompleted` is `async void` with no try/catch: any exception in `BuildServiceProviderAsync`/`ApplyStoredUiSettingsAsync` crashes the process before the window exists, with nothing in the Serilog file log.
  - `BUG-053` - the chat `_persistedShowAiPanel` cache is updated in completion order of concurrent `SelectMany(FromAsync)` writes: two rapid toggles can complete out of order and a subsequent toggle to the stale-cached value is filtered out - the restart restores the old visibility.
  - `BUG-054` - the positions panel catches only `InvalidOperationException`: other cancel/reload failures escape to unobserved `ThrownExceptions` or the window's subscription (stale/empty lists, no error line).
  - `BUG-055` - the positions panel formats stored UTC timestamps as-is while the history window calls `.ToLocalTime()`: the same order shows times differing by the local UTC offset between the two windows.
- **Scope:** `src/ZettaTrade.App/ViewModels/` (`NewOrderDialogViewModel.cs`, `StatusBarViewModel.cs`, `ChatViewModel.cs`, `PositionsPanelViewModel.cs`, `OrderHistoryViewModel.cs`), `src/ZettaTrade.App/App.axaml.cs`.
- **Acceptance:** every listed failure lands as a visible localized error (orders also in the journal); no UI-bound property is written off the UI thread; startup exceptions reach the file log with the fail-fast path explicit; both windows render one timestamp convention (stored UTC -> local, localized).
- **Tests:** per-bug pins (the uncaught-exception scenarios over the VM throw surfaces; a marshaling assert on the timer path; a bootstrap-failure log assert; the timestamp convention held across the two views).
- **Status:** done. Canon: commit `90a0eec` is on feat/xdg-paths; full App.Tests suite passes 314/314 (of which 10 are new: the two BUG-053 facts, the three BUG-054 cases, the BUG-055 convention fact, the two BUG-017 network cases, the BUG-019 log fact and the dispatcher-pump discipline probe) and gate prints `GATE PASSED`. Shipped BUG-017: `HandlePlaceOrderAsync` catches `Exception` (broader than the adapter's validation refusals — the paper adapter reaches its reference rate over the same network surface, so an `HttpRequestException`/`TaskCanceledException` lands exactly like a refused order): one logged entry, the localized `[NewOrder.PlaceFailed: <message>]` line, the dialog stays open and the pipeline re-runnable — no more escape into the unobserved `ReactiveCommand.ThrownExceptions`. Shipped BUG-018: both the fallback-notice application (`UnavailablePersistedChoices.Select(_ => RxVoid.Default).ObserveOnUiThread()`) and its expiry (`Observable.Timer(...).Select(...).ObserveOnUiThread()` inside `ShowFallbackNotice`) marshal onto the Avalonia UI thread — the starter walk's emitting thread and the .NET timer thread never touch the UI-bound `ConnectionStatus` directly. Shipped BUG-019: the startup walk is inside one try/catch; the catch logs `Serilog.Log.Logger.ForContext<App>().Error(exception, ...)` (`App.LogStartupFailure` — the static Serilog logger exists as the composition root's first step, so every store/theme/config failure below it is recordable) and rethrows — the rethrow IS the fail-fast path (the process must not limp on with a half-built service tree); only the `EnsureDirectoriesExist` step before the logger stays unloggable by design (the log directory may be the failed resource; the process error already surfaces it). Shipped BUG-053: the persistence cache LEADS the pipeline (`WhenAnyValue(IsAiChatVisible) → Skip(1) → Where(real difference) → Do(cache := value) → Concat()` of `FromAsync` writes) — `WhenAnyValue` delivers synchronously on the calling thread, so the cache updates in ISSUANCE order and a rapid toggle-pair whose first write is still in flight enqueues the second (a real difference at issue time) instead of being filtered against a stale cache; the writes serialize strictly in issuance order via `.Concat()`, and each write catches its own failure (`SetShowAiPanelAsync`, per-write log) so one dead disk write can no longer terminate the whole persistence pipeline. Shipped BUG-054: the positions panel's reload and cancel catches widened from `InvalidOperationException` to `Exception` with the "same network surface" reasoning as BUG-017 — every failure lands as `[Positions.LoadFailed:*]` / `[Positions.CancelFailed:*]` with the panel re-runnable and no unobserved command exceptions. Shipped BUG-055: both row builders (`CreatePositionRow`, the order-row factory) call `.ToLocalTime()` on the stored UTC stamps — the same convention the order history window already held, so one order renders one wall-clock time in both windows.
- **Tests shipped:** the pipeline-failure pins (dialog placement under `HttpRequestException`/`TaskCanceledException` → localized error, no close; panel reload/cancel under network faults → localized lines, history store untouched; the BUG-053 pair — a first write parked on a gate with the second toggle in flight pins ISSUE-order serialization (`[false, true]` exactly once drained) and a failed first write still persists the second toggle; the BUG-055 cross-view fact — one fixed true-UTC order rendered through BOTH the panel journal and the persistent store compare equal, zone-agnostic; the `LoadCommand_WithData` fact became zone-agnostic too — the time expectation is computed from its own fixture); the BUG-018 marshal pins ride the new deterministic Avalonia dispatcher world — `tests/ZettaTrade.App.Tests/AvaloniaDispatcherPump.cs` installs (once, idempotent, from the static constructors of the three dispatcher-touching classes) a forever-alive owner thread that executes dispatcher jobs ONLY on an explicit `DrainAsync()`, so "posted never executes spontaneously" holds for every fact and the notice facts pin BOTH the marshal (the chip is still `Connected`/empty pre-drain — the pre-fix code wrote inline on the emitting thread) and the value transitions (drain → notice, drain after expiry → re-derived regular text); the dispatcher-touching classes (`ChartViewModelTests`, `OrderBookViewModelTests`, `StatusBarViewModelTests`) run in the serial `AvaloniaDispatcherSerial` collection (their per-class foreign-thread pins are replaced by the shared pump owner); the BUG-019 fact swaps the global `Serilog.Log.Logger` for an in-memory sink, calls `App.LogStartupFailure`, and asserts one ERROR event carrying the exception instance (restored in `finally`).
- **Merge revision:** none expected — App-own VMs, `App.axaml.cs` and App.Tests facts; no protected interfaces, no shared registration files touched.
- **Wave note:** the release version bump + CHANGELOG land once for the whole fixed-wave in a separate release branch after the merges — a supervisor-approved deviation from rule 6 for parallel work.

## T-057 — Risk: market/stop orders bypass the hard order-value cap (BUG-026)

- **Branch:** feat/xdg-paths (base `develop`; queued 2026-11-04, code review 2026-11-04; definitions in `docs/CODE_REVIEW.md`)
- **Origin:** `MaxOrderValueHardCapUsd` is the terminal's last automatic damper against a catastrophic order; its own documentation says it "must never be bypassable by any caller". Today any price-less order bypasses it entirely and relies solely on a human dialog - one fat-fingered market order of any size sails through as a routine flow.
- **What:** `BUG-026` - `DefaultRiskManager` checks `MaxOrderValueHardCapUsd` only for orders with an explicit price; market/stop orders (no estimable value from the request) return `Approved(requiresConfirmation: true)` without any cap check.
- **Scope:** `src/ZettaTrade.App/Services/DefaultRiskManager.cs`.
- **Acceptance:** no order of any type/side slips past the cap; an order without an estimable value is either approximated against the last available price or rejected with an explicit "cannot evaluate risk" error - never silently approved; the estimate-vs-reject decision is agreed with the supervisor at review (both satisfy the rule).
- **Tests:** the cap matrix across order types with and without an explicit price; the reject path's error reporting.
- **Status:** done. Canon: commit `4f97a3b` is on feat/xdg-paths; full App.Tests suite passes 304/304 (the risk matrix now holds 7 facts) and gate prints `GATE PASSED`. Shipped: the estimate-vs-reject decision — REJECTED (fail-closed), agreed-upon by the agent per the "both satisfy the rule" clause, up for supervisor confirmation at review. `DefaultRiskManager.CheckOrderAsync` now anchors the value on the price the REQUEST carries: `Price` for Limit/StopLimit (unchanged) and `StopPrice` for StopMarket (NEW — a stop order valued against ITS OWN trigger now runs the SAME cap/threshold matrix as a limit order: inside-threshold approves plainly, 10k–100k requires confirmation, above the hard cap rejects unconditionally). An order with NEITHER anchor (a market order — per the `OrderRequest` contract its `Price` is null) is REJECTED with the explicit reason "cannot evaluate risk: the order carries no price to estimate its value against the hard cap; place a limit order, or a stop order with its trigger price" — never silently approved, no confirmation-only path an order of any size sails through (the pre-fix `Approved(requiresConfirmation: true)` gone). The manager deliberately gained NO market-data dependency for approximation: a stale last price would estimate nonsense and reopen exactly the bypass this cap exists to prevent (fail-closed is also the codebase's own safety doctrine). The reject path surfaces through both consumers: the order dialog shows the reason string, the MCP `place_order` tool returns it as an error to the model. Facts: the old market-orders-require-confirmation fact replaced by the explicit reject fact (no approval, no confirmation, `cannot evaluate risk` in the reason) plus the stop-trigger matrix (band/confirm, hard-cap/reject); the limit matrix untouched.
- **Merge revision:** none expected — one manager file plus its fact file; the reject-on-market-order UX consequence (market orders need a limit/stop alternative until an estimate source is wired) is a supervisor-visible behavior change called out here.
- **Wave note:** the release version bump + CHANGELOG land once for the whole fixed-wave in a separate release branch after the merges — a supervisor-approved deviation from rule 6 for parallel work.
- **Landed:** merged into develop as merge commit `cbd5dbb` (the whole feat/xdg-paths wave rides one merge); shipped as `0.1.0-alpha.25` on `release/alpha.22-27` (see CHANGELOG.md).

## T-058 — UI primitives: concurrent async helper vs contract, pan pitch mismatch, undisposed SKPath (BUG-025, BUG-048, BUG-049)

- **Branch:** feat/ui-primitives (base `develop` at `46a4bc6`; queued 2026-11-04, code review 2026-11-04; definitions in `docs/CODE_REVIEW.md`)
- **Origin:** one seam, one theme: small contract violations with cumulative cost - serialized semantics silently running concurrently, a pan moving by the wrong distance, native memory churn on every redraw.
- **What:**
  - `BUG-025` - `ObservableAsyncExtensions` documents serialized-in-emission-order execution, but the implementation `.Select(...).Merge()` runs the per-notification tasks concurrently.
  - `BUG-048` - pan pacing computes the bar pitch from `Bounds.Width` while the draw pass uses `plotRect.Width` (bounds minus the measured price gutter): dragging "one bar" moves the window by a different distance than the painted bar width.
  - `BUG-049` - the `SKPath` returned by `path.Detach()` is never disposed (`DrawPath` copies; the wrapper waits for its finalizer): native memory churn on every redraw for every indicator line.
- **Scope:** `src/ZettaTrade.UI/Extensions/ObservableAsyncExtensions.cs`, `src/ZettaTrade.UI/Charting/CandlestickChart.cs`, `src/ZettaTrade.UI/Charting/ChartPanWindow.cs` (the pure `SlotsForDrag` pan-slot seam), plus the matching `ZettaTrade.UI.Tests` files.
- **Acceptance:** the helper runs its per-notification tasks strictly in order (the contract decides the mechanism, e.g. concatenation/queueing instead of `Merge`); pan pacing and painted pitch share one computed constant; the draw pass retains no undisposed `SKPath`.
- **Tests:** the helper's serialization tests (queued runs park until the pending one completes; out-of-order completions never reorder the starts); the pan-parity asserts (one painted bar = one slot, measured through `ChartBarLayout` — the same layout the draw pass paints with); the path disposal rides the structural `using` scope at the single `Detach` site (the suite has no headless SKSurface harness — a leak assert would race the finalizer).
- **Status:** done. Canon: the branch carries `f12c8a9` (the three fixes + their tests: 9 new asserts) and release commit `6a1a59a` (the alpha.30 cut + the status flip); shipped as `0.1.0-alpha.30` (see CHANGELOG.md). Gate passed with `-AllowProtected` (the `Directory.Build.props` bump is the supervisor-only protected file).

## T-059 — Themes: burst hot-reload drops files, watcher death, unordered live list, stale order book (BUG-046, BUG-047, BUG-050)

- **Branch:** feat/theme-hot-reload (base `develop` at `6a1a59a`; queued 2026-11-04, code review 2026-11-04; definitions in `docs/CODE_REVIEW.md`)
- **Origin:** the hot-reload is a headline feature (edit your theme file, see it live) and its failure modes are silent (files dropped, the watcher dead, no error anywhere); a live-list race is a crash risk on every theme enumeration.
- **What:**
  - `BUG-046` - one global `Throttle` collapses a burst spanning different theme files into a single event: only the last-changed file reloads, others changed in the same 150 ms window never do; the FSW `Error` handler only logs: after an internal-buffer overflow the watcher stops raising events and hot-reload silently dies.
  - `BUG-047` - `AvailableThemes` returns the live `List<ThemeInfo>` that the background hot-reload pipeline mutates under `_lock`: an unsynchronized consumer enumerating it throws `InvalidOperationException`.
  - `BUG-050` - `OrderBookWidget` never subscribes to `ThemeChanged`/`LanguageChanged` (unlike the chart): after a theme or language switch the drawn book (colors, column headers) stays stale until the next `Book` snapshot arrives - indefinitely while disconnected.
- **Scope:** `src/Livery/Themes/` (`ThemeFileWatcher.cs`, `ThemeManager.cs` — their home since the T-065 Livery extraction, the queued path naming predates the move), `src/ZettaTrade.UI/Charting/OrderBookWidget.cs`, `src/Livery/Livery.csproj` (one `InternalsVisibleTo` for the test seam).
- **Acceptance:** a burst over N files reloads all N; a watcher error restarts the watch (a logged, not silent, failure); `AvailableThemes` hands out an immutable snapshot; the order book re-renders live on theme and language changes.
- **Tests:** the burst integration test (a burst over two files reloads every one); the restart pin through the internal `RestartForever` seam (an error re-watches with a fresh source lap and logs the failure); the `AvailableThemes` copy-isolation pin (a snapshot survives the pipeline's in-place mutation); the existing hot-reload suites ride along. The queued "widget render test on theme/language change" wording resolved into the chart-convention wiring (attach-scoped subscriptions + per-render snapshot) — the widget has no headless render harness to drive, the assert rides the existing widget suites.
- **Status:** done. Canon: the branch carries `ef67399` (the three fixes + their tests: 3 new tests; the registration is an immutable reference-swapped snapshot, so the `AvailableThemes` read never blocks) and release commit `8bc1d1f` (the alpha.31 cut + the status flip); shipped as `0.1.0-alpha.31` (see CHANGELOG.md).

## T-060 — Localization: culture race in the cached lookup + suspected binding lifetime leak (BUG-051, BUG-052 suspected)

- **Branch:** feat/xdg-paths (base `develop`; queued 2026-11-04, code review 2026-11-04; definitions in `docs/CODE_REVIEW.md`)
- **Origin:** the language switch is a runtime feature across the whole terminal; the cache race hands a racing caller a string in the wrong culture; BUG-052 is a suspected leak - investigate first, then fix or close with evidence.
- **What:**
  - `BUG-051` - `GetString` reads the cache key from `_currentCulture` before `GetOrAdd`: a concurrent language switch clears the cache and a racing caller receives a string in the wrong culture (self-heals on the next switch).
  - `BUG-052` (suspected) - the `{loc:Localized}` observable chain (`GetObservable(...)` -> `ToBinding()`) may keep detached/abandoned controls rooted for the app lifetime: VERIFY Avalonia binding weak-reference semantics BEFORE treating this as a confirmed leak.
- **Scope:** `src/ZettaTrade.UI/Localization/` (`LocalizationManager.cs`, `LocalizedExtension.cs`).
- **Acceptance:** a switching storm yields no wrong-culture string at a caller (the cache key is a per-call snapshot); BUG-052 resolved with a documented verdict (fixed, or closed as a false positive with the evidence attached below).
- **Tests:** a concurrency stress (N callers x rapid switches); if the leak confirms, an instance-count/weak-reference probe.
- **Status:** done. Canon: commit `3885347` is on feat/xdg-paths; full UI.Tests suite passes 161/161 (the localization matrix now holds 3 facts, of which 3 are new — the manager had no direct fact file before) and gate prints `GATE PASSED`. Shipped BUG-051: `LocalizationManager.GetString` snapshots `_currentCulture` ONCE per call and the snapshot feeds BOTH the cache key (`"{culture.Name}:{key}"`) AND the resource factory (`GetStringFromResource(key, culture)` takes the culture as its argument); the pre-fix code read the field at two levels (the key read inline, the factory re-read `_currentCulture` for the lookup), so a language switch landing between the reads stored a wrong-culture value under the caller's key where it stayed until the next cache clear; a racing caller may still receive either the pre-switch or the post-switch string, but always the one consistent with its own snapshot. Shipped BUG-052 verdict: **CLOSED AS A FALSE POSITIVE — no leak; no code change**, with the evidence: in Avalonia 12.1.4 (the exact tag the app pins) the observable-to-binding path is `AvaloniaObjectExtensions.ToBinding` → private `BindingAdaptor.CreateInstance` → `new UntypedObservableBindingExpression(_source, BindingPriority.LocalValue)` (`src/Avalonia.Base/AvaloniaObjectExtensions.cs`), and that expression, on `StartCore`, subscribes the app-lifetime chain through `WeakObserverSubscription<object?>.Subscribe(_observable, this)` (`src/Avalonia.Base/Data/Core/UntypedObservableBindingExpression.cs`); `WeakObserverSubscription` (`src/Avalonia.Base/Reactive/WeakObserverSubscription.cs`) holds "only a weak reference to the observer, disposing the subscription once the observer has been collected" and self-unsubscribes on the next emission once the observer is collected — so the singleton `_languageChanged` subject chain keeps NO strong edge into the binding expression; the control is the rooter (control → property value frame → binding expression) and an abandoned control becomes unreachable, the weak subscription then dies at the next `OnNext` — an app-lifetime leak rooting detached controls would require a strong subject→expression edge, which 12.1.4 does not have; the per-binding intermediate operator chain (Defer → Concat → DistinctUntilChanged) is short-lived bookkeeping rooted only while the binding lives, not a control sink. The instance-count probe was therefore not needed (the acceptance requires it only when the leak confirms).
- **Tests shipped:** the stamping-glue pin (every lookup carries the culture of its cache key, before and after a switch), the deterministic mid-flight case (a factory parked across a committed switch must return ITS snapshot's string — `en:Menu.File` — while the manager is already on `ru`, and the post-switch lookups stay clean of the parked call's re-added entry) and the switching storm (4 reader loops × 150 lookups over 2 keys against 24 rapid switches; the post-storm sweep pins every key resolving to the final culture's stamp — a persistent mixed-culture entry would show). The facts run through a culture-stamping fake `ResourceManager` (every lookup returns `"{lookupCulture}:{name}"`, making any key/value mixture directly observable) with a park hook for the deterministic case; ambient cultures are restored per fact; `ChartTickCultureTests` and `LocalizationManagerTests` were put in the shared `LocalizationManagerSingleton` xUnit collection — every manager construction re-registers the static `Instance`, and `ChartTickCulture.Resolve` resolves through it, so the whole minefield must run strictly sequentially (the interference showed up live during this task's test runs).
- **Wave note:** the release version bump + CHANGELOG land once for the whole fixed-wave in a separate release branch after the merges — a supervisor-approved deviation from rule 6 for parallel work.
- **Landed:** merged into develop as merge commit `cbd5dbb` (the whole feat/xdg-paths wave rides one merge); shipped as `0.1.0-alpha.26` on `release/alpha.22-27` (see CHANGELOG.md).

## T-061 — Order history store: terminal-status rewrite + upsert check-then-act race (BUG-042, BUG-043)

- **Branch:** feat/xdg-paths (base `develop`; queued 2026-11-04, code review 2026-11-04; definitions in `docs/CODE_REVIEW.md`)
- **Origin:** the order history is the journal of record for trading activity; rewriting a terminal status falsifies the journal (an order marked Cancelled that actually Filled), and the upsert race surfaces as UNIQUE-constraint errors into the callers.
- **What:**
  - `BUG-042` - `MarkCancelledAsync` unconditionally sets `Status = Cancelled`: a late/duplicate cancel for an order already `Filled`/`Rejected`/`Expired` rewrites the history instead of being ignored.
  - `BUG-043` - the upsert is `FindAsync` then `Add` without a real DB upsert: two concurrent `RecordPlacedAsync` calls for the same order both see null and the second `SaveChangesAsync` fails with a UNIQUE-constraint error instead of updating.
- **Scope:** `src/ZettaTrade.Infrastructure/Persistence/SqliteOrderHistoryStore.cs`.
- **Acceptance:** terminal statuses are final (a cancel attempt on a terminal order is ignored, never a silent rewrite); the placement is a true DB upsert - concurrent `RecordPlacedAsync` calls never surface constraint errors.
- **Tests:** the status-transition matrix (each terminal status x a cancel attempt); the concurrent place/cancel scenario.
- **Status:** done. Canon: commit `49e9840` is on feat/xdg-paths; full Infrastructure.Tests suite passes 114/114 (the store matrix now holds 14 facts, of which 6 changed/are new) and gate prints `GATE PASSED`. Shipped: BUG-043 — `RecordPlacedAsync` became ONE raw statement, `INSERT INTO Orders (...) VALUES (...) ON CONFLICT (ExchangeId, OrderId) DO UPDATE SET <all 15 non-key columns>` via `ExecuteSqlInterpolatedAsync` (EF parameterizes the interpolated values automatically): insert-or-update is decided atomically on the database side against the composite primary key, so the check-then-act `FindAsync`/`Add` window — two concurrent placements both seeing null, the second `SaveChangesAsync` dying on the unique constraint — is closed; the interpolated SQL runs AROUND the EF converters, so every value is pre-converted through THE SAME converter the entity model keeps on that column (the four `OrderHistoryConversions.{Timestamp,NullableTimestamp,Amount,NullableAmount}` converters compiled once into static `Func`s via `ConvertToProviderExpression().Compile()`, enums `ToString()` matching the `HasConversion<string>()` of the model) — the stored TEXT stays byte-consistent whether a row was written by `SaveChanges` or by the upsert; BUG-042 — a private `IsTerminal(OrderStatus)` (Filled/Cancelled/Rejected/Expired) guards `MarkCancelledAsync`: a cancel for a row whose recorded status is terminal logs a warning ("...arrived after the terminal status {Status}; the recorded outcome is kept") and leaves the row untouched (status and UpdatedAt stay as placed); the now-dead `ToEntry`/`OverwriteEntry` private helpers are deleted (`ToOrder` keeps serving reads). Facts: the two cancel facts corrected to a live-order precondition (`Pending`, not the helper's default `Filled` — the restart story too), the terminal matrix (Filled/Cancelled/Rejected/Expired × cancel ⇒ status kept, `UpdatedAt` null ⇒ no rewrite, single row), the concurrent-placement fact (two store instances on the SAME file, `Task.WhenAll` of two `RecordPlacedAsync` for the same order ⇒ no throw, `ContainSingle`, last-commit-wins status ∈ {Pending, Filled}) and the place/cancel race smoke (one store, `Task.WhenAll(place, cancel)` ⇒ no throw, one consistent row ∈ {Pending, Cancelled}).
- **Merge revision:** none expected — one store file plus its fact file; no protected or shared surfaces touched.
- **Wave note:** the release version bump + CHANGELOG land once for the whole fixed-wave in a separate release branch after the merges — a supervisor-approved deviation from rule 6 for parallel work.
- **Landed:** merged into develop as merge commit `cbd5dbb` (the whole feat/xdg-paths wave rides one merge); shipped as `0.1.0-alpha.23` on `release/alpha.22-27` (see CHANGELOG.md).

## T-062 — XDG path resolver returns CWD-relative paths for empty environment variables (BUG-044)

- **Branch:** feat/xdg-paths (base `develop`; queued 2026-11-04, code review 2026-11-04; definitions in `docs/CODE_REVIEW.md`)
- **Origin:** the Linux platform contract (XDG Base Directory Specification) requires the default when a variable is empty; today an empty `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`/`XDG_DATA_HOME`/`XDG_STATE_HOME` silently produces CWD-relative paths - broken locations in containers and scripts that set variables without values.
- **What:** `BUG-044` - `ConfigPathResolver` falls back only on null (`??`), so an empty-string variable fails the fallback: `Path.Combine` then resolves against the current working directory.
- **Scope:** `src/ZettaTrade.Infrastructure/Configuration/ConfigPathResolver.cs`.
- **Acceptance:** unset OR empty environment variables both fall back to the platform defaults; a non-empty variable wins as before.
- **Tests:** the env matrix (unset / empty / valid) pinned per resolved directory.
- **Status:** done. Canon: commit `12564fc` is on feat/xdg-paths; full Infrastructure.Tests suite passes 122/122 (the resolver matrix now holds 8 facts, of which 8 are new — the class had no test file before) and gate prints `GATE PASSED`. Shipped: the three resolvers' XDG fallback moved off the null-only `??` onto one shared rule — `ResolveXdgRoot(envValue, defaultRoot)`: null, empty AND whitespace-only values all fall back to the platform default (whitespace is counted as unset as well — it is a relative path the application could never create nor write through; strictly stronger than the acceptance's "empty"), while a non-empty value wins as before; each resolver still reads ITS OWN variable raw and passes ITS OWN spec default (`XDG_CONFIG_HOME`→`~/.config`, `XDG_DATA_HOME`→`~/.local/share`, `XDG_STATE_HOME`→`~/.local/state`), the app-name suffix lives in the shared helper. The facts surface: the OS-guarded resolvers never reach their XDG branches on a Windows CI process, so three internal one-line seams (`LinuxConfigRoot/LinuxDataRoot/LinuxStateRoot(envValue)`) stand in for the process environment — the exact code production runs — and the facts call them: the broken-values Theory (null / empty / whitespace) pins all three directories' own defaults, the valid-value fact keeps a non-empty override winning, the shared-rule Theory pins the fallback composition itself, and the Windows-surface fact pins the roaming/local profile split (`config` under `%APPDATA%`, `data`+`logs` under `%LOCALAPPDATA%`) so the refactor cannot drift the non-XDG roots.
- **Merge revision:** none expected — one resolver file plus a new fact file.
- **Wave note:** the release version bump + CHANGELOG land once for the whole fixed-wave in a separate release branch after the merges — a supervisor-approved deviation from rule 6 for parallel work.
- **Landed:** merged into develop as merge commit `cbd5dbb` (the whole feat/xdg-paths wave rides one merge); shipped as `0.1.0-alpha.24` on `release/alpha.22-27` (see CHANGELOG.md).
